This proverb has been traced back to the 1550s-1560s, when its form was 'Use makes perfect.' The Latin version is: 'Uses promptos facit.'<br _ngcontent-serverapp-c8=""/><br _ngcontent-serverapp-c8=""/> So now you know. </p></div><div _ngcontent-serverapp-c8="" bp="5@md hide show@sm" class="zoomy-image"><div _ngcontent-serverapp-c8="" class="it-image practice-it-image"></div></div></div></div><div _ngcontent-serverapp-c8="" bp="8@xl 9@lg 12@md" class="card card-hover-shadow"><div _ngcontent-serverapp-c8="" bp="grid gap-none"><div _ngcontent-serverapp-c8="" bp="5@md hide show@sm" class="zoomy-image"><div _ngcontent-serverapp-c8="" class="it-image prove-it-image"></div></div><div _ngcontent-serverapp-c8="" bp="7@md" class="card-content"><span _ngcontent-serverapp-c8="" class="pre-heading">Prove IT</span><h4 _ngcontent-serverapp-c8="">Show off your skills</h4><p _ngcontent-serverapp-c8=""> While you might be happy basking in the knowledge that you're an IT supremo, how do you make other people appreciate that?
